# BSCraft 4 guide
-# Minecraft 1.20.1, Forge 47.4.10, 256 mods. Server: `bscraft.zukashix.com`

Hey everyone.

This is a guide to what's in BSCraft 4. It covers exploring (biomes, structures, dungeons and the extra dimensions), fighting (bosses, weapons, armor, enchantments), magic (the two spell systems and all their addons), the creatures you'll run into, the tech mods, food and building, and it lists every mod in the pack. Stats for weapons, armor and bosses are included where we could get them.

A couple of things before you read it.

This isn't a walkthrough. The pack has no set order and nobody's going to tell you what to do first, so play however you want. The guide is just here so you know what exists.

It also doesn't have everything. We went through every mod to put this together and there's still stuff we haven't found. With this many mods in one pack, things interact in ways nobody planned, and some of it you'll only come across by exploring. If you find something that isn't in here, post it in the server.

The stats were taken from the pack's own files, so they should match our version. If an update changes something, trust the in-game tooltip over this.

**Sections**
> Basics: what BSCraft 4 is, what's different from vanilla, quality of life, controls, settings worth changing, what you can do, difficulty tiers, ways people play
> Exploring: biomes, structures and loot, the Nether and the End, the Twilight Forest, Alex's Caves, space and vehicles, ores
> Fighting: bosses, weapons, ranged weapons and gadgets, armor, enchantments, powers and accessories, how to get gear
> Magic: how magic works here, Ars Nouveau, Iron's Spells 'n Spellbooks
> Creatures: creatures, mounts and companions, the bestiary
> Tech: Create and power, RFTools, how the bigger systems work
> Everyday stuff: food and drink, building and gadgets
> The full mod list, and where to read more

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# What BSCraft 4 is
BSCraft 4 is an adventure pack set in a fantasy world. The world has 150+ new biomes, new cave biomes underneath, and around 20 structure mods, so there are dungeons, castles, ruins, towns, wizard towers and boss lairs pretty much everywhere. There are extra dimensions (the Twilight Forest, a rebuilt Nether and End, and a few planets), 40+ bosses, a lot of new creatures, and dozens of new weapons and armor sets.

Magic is a big part of it. Two full magic systems run side by side and share one mana bar. In Ars Nouveau you build your own spells out of glyphs, and use rituals, familiars and helper creatures to automate things. In Iron's Spells 'n Spellbooks you find spells as scrolls, level them up and cast them from a spellbook. Around 25 addons add more schools, bosses, armor and gear to both.

There's a tech side too if you want it. Create and its addons cover machines, trains, power, aircraft, ships and rockets, and RFTools adds teleporters, quarries and custom dimensions. Some of it crosses over with magic (Ars Creo and Ars Technica), but you can go deep into any part, mix them, or skip one completely.

There's no questline, and nothing forces you down one path.

## The quality-of-life stuff that matters most
- **Loot is per player** (Lootr): every chest, barrel and minecart that came with the world has its own loot for each player. A dungeon someone else already cleared still has loot waiting for you, and nobody can take yours.
- **You keep your stuff when you die** (Corpse): everything you carried goes into a corpse where you died, and it never despawns while there's anything in it. `Shift` + `U` shows where it is.
- **Free fast travel** (Waystones): teleporting between waystones you've found costs nothing, and you can jump to one straight from the world map.
- **Villagers are people** (Minecraft Comes Alive): they have names, faces, families and moods. They still trade the same way, and villages now have guards.
- **Look anything up** (JEI): `R` on an item shows how to make it, `U` shows what it's used for.
- **Less inventory hassle**: backpacks, one-click sorting, hotbar auto-refill, a totem slot, an Elytra slot and accessory slots.

The Quality of life section has all the details.

## Best in slot, at a glance
- **Melee, raw hit**: the Glass Sword (40 damage, but only 1 use), then Spirit Crusher (18), the Boreal Blade (16), and Whisper of the Abyss, Hearthflame or Soulkeeper (14). From the Twilight Forest, Magic From The East, Iron's Spells, Aquamirae and Simply Swords.
- **Melee, sustained**: Bramblethorn, Molten Edge, Wickpiercer, and the Runic katana, twinblade or cutlass lead at around 18 to 20 damage per second, plus their abilities. Iron's Amethyst Rapier, Hellrazor, Twilight Gale and Spellbreaker are right behind at about 18. From Simply Swords and Iron's Spells.
- **Armor, raw**: Adam's Void armor (34 armor, 6 toughness per piece) is the strongest in the pack, but it's the last step of the Adam's Ars Plus boss chain. Before that, Aeternium (24 armor, 3.5 toughness, 0.2 knockback res) from BetterEnd beats netherite on every stat.
- **Armor, specialist**: Heavy (4.0 toughness, 0.5 knockback res) gives knockback immunity, Fiery (24 armor) gives raw armor, and Abyssal has a built-in death save. From Immersive Armors, Twilight Forest and Aquamirae.
- **Magic**: a full Iron's school set (+500 mana, +20% spell power, +40% to one school) or Dread Robes for Blood magic (+80% Blood power), with an Ancient Codex for shorter cooldowns. On the Ars side, tier 3 robes with Spell Power threads, or Ars Elemental armor for one element. The Doppelganger Ring (+30 HP, +150 mana) is the best single ring. From Iron's Spells, Alex's Caves Spellbooks, Ars Nouveau, Ars Elemental and Dark Doppelganger.
- **Ranged**: Dreadbow (30-arrow rain), Raygun, Triple Bow and Extinction Spear cover crowd-clear, beam DPS, i-frame bypass and a returning spear. From Alex's Caves and Twilight Forest.
- **Flight**: the Aeternium Armored Elytra, an RFTools Flight field, the Ars Ritual of Flight or Gliding thread, the Cloud Steps thread, Iron's Angel Wings spell, dragons, and aircraft. From BetterEnd, RFTools, Ars Nouveau, Adam's Ars Plus, Iron's Spells, Dragon Mounts and Immersive Aircraft.
- **Death insurance**: a Totem in the charm slot, the Twilight Forest's Charm of Life or Charm of Keeping, the Ars Undying thread, a Soulbound enchant, or your corpse itself, which holds everything you carried and never despawns while it has items in it. Curio slot, Twilight Forest, Ars Nouveau, Ensorcellation.
- **Endgame utility**: the Dimensional Carver teleports you home, the Environmental Controller buffs your whole base, the Transmutation Table converts items, and Ars Starbuncles and Wixies run your item moving and crafting. From Alex's Mobs, RFTools and Ars Nouveau.

# What's different from vanilla
If you're coming from vanilla, or even from other modpacks, a lot works differently here. Settings marked *(this pack)* are specific to BSCraft's configuration, so even players who know these mods will find some of it different.

- **The world**: 150+ new biomes (Oh The Biomes We've Gone, Regions Unexplored, Nyctophobia). Vanilla villages are gone, replaced by sprawling Epic Villages, Towns & Towers, and floating Tidal Towns. Dungeons, mineshafts, strongholds, temples, fortresses and monuments are all redesigned (YUNG's), and about 20 structure mods add castles, sky fortresses, taverns, sunken ships and lairs.
- **Underground**: six hidden Alex's Caves biomes sit beneath the Overworld, each hundreds of blocks wide with its own ecosystem, boss and gear, plus new cave biomes from YUNG's (frosted, sandy, marble).
- **Nether and End**: both are rebuilt, with new biomes, ores, mobs and metals. The End island is redesigned and the Ender Dragon has new attacks (dives, a laser, crystal guardians, about 300 HP). A second portal type links the End back to the Overworld.
- **New dimensions**: the Twilight Forest, four planets plus Earth orbit (Moon, Mars, Mercury, Venus), and dimensions you design yourself with RFTools.
- **Mobs**: 150+ new creatures and 40+ bosses. Vanilla mobs behave differently too: skeletons turn into Barbed in water, Drowned walk on land at night, zombies come in a dozen variants, and Skreechers can summon a Warden.
- **Combat**: 15 weapon types (katanas, halberds, greathammers and more) and around 40 legendary weapons with active abilities. Shields can parry but go on cooldown. There's a dodge roll (`Z`), 38 extra enchantments, and higher attribute caps so big stats actually work.
- **Villagers**: every villager is a person with a name, a face, a family and a mood (Minecraft Comes Alive). They still take jobs and trade normally. Some of them are village guards.
- **Woodcutting**: breaking one log fells the whole tree, and saplings that drop on the ground plant themselves.
- **Magic**: two full magic systems, Ars Nouveau and Iron's Spells 'n Spellbooks, plus around 25 addons, all sharing one mana bar (Ars 'n Spells). Wizard towers, catacombs and mage dungeons show up in the world, and scrolls, ink and Arcane Essence turn up in ordinary chests.
- **Your character**: accessory (curio) slots for rings, belts, charms and more (`G`). An Elytra slot, so you can wear a chestplate and wings together. A Totem of Undying works from the charm slot. Food gives buffs (Comfort, Nourishment, Strength and others), and in this pack vanilla stews give Nourishment too *(this pack)*.
- **Death**: your items (curios included) wait for you in a corpse instead of scattering, and a death-history screen shows where you fell. Full corpses never despawn.
- **Loot**: every chest is per-player (Lootr), so everyone gets their own roll. Mob spawners shut off after a while unless redstone-powered. Any chest except village houses can hide a legendary weapon, and some "chests" are Mimics. The Quality of life section explains how it works.
- **Tech**: Create's rotational machines replace most redstone automation: trains, contraptions, factories, nuclear reactors, electricity and computers. Aircraft recipes go through Create's mechanical crafter *(this pack)*. Redstone dust uses a faster engine with a slightly different update order.
- **Travel**: waystone teleports are free *(this pack)*. Also: planes, airships, physics ships, submarines, trains, rockets, and dragons.
- **Sleeping**: sleeping bags skip the night without moving your spawn, and hammocks skip the day.

## Settings unique to this pack
- The Alex's Caves nuclear bomb has a 48-block radius, about 33% bigger than stock, and the Totem of Possession works on players.
- Alex's Caves mobs spawn 1.75 times as densely, and each cave biome needs its own decoded Codex before its map works.
- Dragon eggs only come from the Ender Dragon, and a fresh one appears after every kill.
- Twilight Forest progression locks are on, and bosses drop their loot into a chest where they spawned.
- Aircraft bombs and crashes don't break blocks.
- Four Graveyard mobs are disabled (Acolyte, Wraith, Corrupted Pillager, Corrupted Vindicator). The Void Worm can only be summoned.
- Chunk loaders switch off after 7 days offline. A Simply Swords legendary can drop from any non-village chest (0.1% per roll), and the Ender Dragon has a 50% chance to drop one.
- The server runs on Normal difficulty with PvP on, and there's no spawn protection.
- Iron's Spells shows your mana bar all the time. Wind's Spellbooks' Tornado spell is disabled because it crashed servers, and Ars Additions' chunkloading ritual is disabled.
- The pack fixes two broken Iron's Spells loot tables, so Catacombs crypt chests give their bonus ink and the Citadel's lore books have their text.
- Adam's Ars Plus Mysterious Mages can break blocks, and they can spawn in any dimension.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Quality of life
A lot of this pack is set up so you spend your time exploring and fighting, not fighting the game. Most of it works on its own, but it's worth knowing what's there, because some of it changes how you play.

## Loot is per player (Lootr)
- Every loot container that comes with the world gives each player their own loot. That's chests, trapped chests, barrels, shulker boxes in End Cities and chest minecarts in mineshafts, in every structure mod. When you open one, you get your own roll, and nobody else can take it.
- So it doesn't matter if a friend already cleared a dungeon, or if you're the tenth person to walk through a ruin. Every chest in it still has loot for you, as long as you haven't opened that one yourself. Going back through places other people explored is always worth it.
- Everyone's loot is rolled separately, so you and a friend won't get the same items from the same chest.
- The Elytra in an End Ship works the same way, so every player can get their own from the same ship.
- Once you've opened a chest, your loot in it stays as you left it. It doesn't refill over time and it doesn't crumble away (both are turned off in this pack), so you can even leave things in it and come back later.
- Chests you've already opened look different to you, so you can tell at a glance what you haven't checked yet in a dungeon.
- Loot chests can't be broken by accident. You have to sneak while breaking one, and breaking it deletes your loot in it, so only do that on purpose.
- Chests you place yourself are normal chests. Only the ones that came with the world work like this.
- There are advancements for opening 1, 10, 25, 50, 100 and 1,337 loot containers.
- Dungeon mob spawners shut off after a while (FixMySpawnR), so a dungeon someone already went through is usually quieter. A redstone signal keeps a spawner running if you want a mob farm.

## Dying doesn't wipe your stuff (Corpse)
- When you die, everything you were carrying goes into a corpse where you died: armor, hotbar, inventory and your curio slots. Nothing scatters on the ground and nothing despawns.
- A corpse with items in it never despawns. Lava doesn't destroy it, and if you die in the void it doesn't fall out of the world. Once it's empty it disappears after 30 seconds. After an hour it turns into a skeleton, which only changes how it looks.
- Press `Shift` + `U` for your death history: where each death happened and what you had on you. Xaero's map also drops a death waypoint so you can walk back, and removes it once you get there.
- By default anyone can open your corpse, so a friend can pick up your gear and bring it to you. It also means someone else could take it, and PvP is on on the server, so don't leave it lying around.
- Items with Curse of Vanishing don't go into the corpse.
- To avoid dying in the first place: a Totem of Undying works from the charm slot (`G`) without holding it, the Twilight Forest's Charm of Life saves you from one death, and the Charm of Keeping or the Soulbound enchant keep items on you when you respawn.
- After you respawn, log in or change dimension you get up to 50 seconds of protection from damage (Logprot). Fall damage still counts.

-# These are the Corpse defaults. The server pack doesn't change them, but an admin could.

## Getting around
- **Waystones**: teleporting between waystones you've found is free. Only the warp button in your inventory (5 minute cooldown) and warps to another dimension (3 levels) cost anything. Sharestones link everyone who uses the same color, which makes a good server hub.
- **Where waystones are**: roughly one every 24 chunks out in the world, built as a blossom arch, a moonlight gazebo or a fairy ring in materials that match the biome. Villages have their own. They also generate in the Nether, the End, the Twilight Forest, the Graveyard's past and on all four Northstar planets.
- **Travel from the map** (Fast Travel Waypoints): open the world map (`M`), click a waystone you've found and you travel there. It costs nothing, including between dimensions. You have to be standing within 7 blocks of a waystone to use it, so it replaces the long waystone list rather than letting you teleport from anywhere.
- **Maps**: Xaero's minimap and world map (`M`) with waypoints (`Shift` + `B`), and the map shows structures you've found.
- **Finding things**: Nature's Compass finds any biome by name. Cartographers sell explorer maps, and several mods add their own maps and compasses to their structures.
- **Elytra slot**: an Elytra has its own slot, so you can wear it with a chestplate.
- **Traveler's Titles**: the name of each biome and dimension shows up on screen when you walk into it.
- **Sleeping**: Comforts sleeping bags skip the night without moving your spawn point, and hammocks skip the day.

## Woodcutting, planting and light
- **Whole trees fall** (FallingTree): break one log and the rest of the tree comes down with it, leaves included. It works on nether fungus and mangrove roots too. Sneak while you break a log to chop normally instead. A tree stops at 100 logs, so giant jungle trees can need a second swing.
- **Saplings plant themselves** (Saplanting): a sapling lying on the ground plants itself about two seconds later, if there's room around it. Forests grow back without you doing anything, and the leftovers from felling a tree turn into the next one.
- **Lanterns you can wear** (Curious Lanterns): put a lantern in your belt slot (`G`) and it lights the area around you, so you keep your off-hand free. It also adds more lantern styles to build with.

## Inventory and storage
- **Backpacks** (Sophisticated Backpacks, `B`): upgradeable from leather to netherite, with upgrades for a magnet, auto-feeding, auto-refill, crafting, smelting, voiding junk, bigger stacks and more.
- **Sorting and refill** (Inventory Profiles Next): a sort button on inventories and chests, and your hotbar refills itself when a stack runs out or a tool is about to break.
- **Moving items** (Mouse Tweaks): drag across slots and scroll the mouse wheel to move items quickly.
- **Accessory slots** (Curios, `G`): rings, necklaces, belts, charms and more, worn on top of your armor.
- **Bigger chests** (Iron Chests), chunk loaders to keep farms running (they switch off after 7 days offline), and Polymorph to pick the output when two recipes clash.

## Knowing what you're looking at
- **JEI**: every recipe (`R`) and every use (`U`) for any item, including this pack's changed recipes. When a wiki and JEI disagree, JEI is right.
- **JER**: ore heights, mob drops and structure loot inside JEI.
- **Jade**: shows what block or mob you're looking at (`Numpad 1` to toggle).
- **Mob Plaques** (`Shift` + `J`): health and armor above mobs. **AppleSkin**: hunger and saturation preview.
- **Tooltips**: Enchantment Descriptions explains every enchant on its book, and Legendary Tooltips marks rare items.
- **Inventory HUD** (`I`): your armor, held items and potion timers on screen.
- **BetterF3**: a cleaner F3 screen for coordinates, biome and light level.
- **In-game books**: several mods have their own guide book (Ars Nouveau's is the best one to read early).

## Safer and smoother
- **Combat**: a dodge roll (`Z`, Bosses'Rise), shield parries (Shield Expansion), and higher attribute caps (AttributeFix), so big armor and damage numbers actually count.
- **Clean Swing Through Grass**: you can hit mobs standing in tall grass.
- **Sit** on stairs and slabs.
- **Magic**: one shared mana bar for both magic systems, always on screen, so you don't juggle two resources.
- **Server login**: the server runs in offline mode, so Simple Login protects your player name with a password that your game stores and sends when you join. If you can't get in after reinstalling or switching PCs, that's usually why, so ask an admin.

# Controls and info tools
It helps to learn the lookup tools early. JEI shows every recipe (including the pack's aircraft recipes, which don't match what the wikis say), JER shows ore heights and mob drops, and Jade tells you what you're looking at.

These are the pack's default keybinds. If you installed the pack before the magic update, your own settings were kept, so your keys might be different. Check Options, then Controls, if something doesn't work.

## Looking things up
- JEI recipe / uses (hover an item in your inventory): `R` / `U`
- Search JEI: `Ctrl` + `F`
- Show or hide JEI: `Ctrl` + `O`
- Ponder (Create's animated help): hold `W` while hovering a Create item
- Jade block info: `Numpad 1` to toggle, hold `Left Shift` for more details
- Mod manual (for mods that have one): `F1`

## Map
- World map: `M`
- Waypoint list: `U`
- New waypoint: `Shift` + `B`
- Instant waypoint: `Numpad +`
- Bigger minimap: `Shift` + `Z`
- Minimap settings: `F7`
- Travel to a waystone: stand within 7 blocks of any waystone, open the world map (`M`) and click another waystone you've found

## Villagers
- Talk to a villager: right-click them. That opens the screen with their mood, family and everything you can do with them.
- Trade: the Trade button in that screen, not a separate key.

## Inventory and gear
- Wear a lantern for light: put it in the belt slot on the accessories screen (`G`)
- Accessories (curios): `G`
- Open backpack: `B`
- Sort a backpack or storage: middle mouse
- Backpack upgrade toggles: `Alt` + `Z`, `Alt` + `X`
- Quiver: `Shift` + `V`
- Death history (Corpse): `Shift` + `U`
- Special armor ability: `Y`
- Inventory HUD on screen: `I`, settings with `Shift` + `O`

## Magic
- Iron's Spells, cast your active spell: `V`
- Iron's Spells, spell wheel: `R`
- Iron's Spells, scroll the spell bar: hold `Left Alt` and scroll
- Ars Nouveau, open the spell book menu: `N`
- Ars Nouveau, next / previous spell slot: `X` / `Shift` + `X`
- Ars Nouveau, use your head curio: `Shift` + `N`
- Ars Zero, cast from a curio / curio menu: `H` / `P`
- Ars Elemental, open the curio pouch: `J`
- Spellbook lore pages (GTBC): left and right arrow keys

## Combat and movement
- Dodge roll (Bosses'Rise): `Z`
- Zoom: `C`
- Swap offhand: `F`
- Elytra flight mode (Do a Barrel Roll): `Shift` + `I` to toggle. While it's on and you're flying, `A` / `D` turn and `W` gives thrust.

## Riding and vehicles
- Aircraft boost / dismount: `B` / `R`
- Ship cruise / go down (Valkyrien Skies): `F` / `V`
- Dragon: `Z` to go down, `F6` to switch camera flight
- Machinery horn: `H`
- Create tool menus and train menus: `Left Alt`
- Clockwork goggles: `Shift` + `G`

## Visuals
- Shaders on or off: `K`
- Pick a shader pack: `O`
- Reload shaders: `F8`
- Mob health plaques: `Shift` + `J`
- Footstep sound settings: `F10`

> A few keys do two things, but only in different situations. `R` is the spell wheel, and also dismount while you're in an aircraft. `V` casts, and also moves a ship down while you're piloting one. `F` swaps your offhand, and also cruises a ship. `Z` is dodge roll, and also takes a dragon down while you're riding it. `Left Alt` scrolls Iron's spells, but near a Create toolbox it can also open the toolbox menu. `H` is the Ars Zero curio cast and also the machinery horn inside a vehicle.

> The quality-of-life mods (Lootr, Corpse, Waystones, backpacks and the rest) have their own section, right before this one.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Settings worth changing
The pack ships with safe defaults. These are the settings most people end up changing, with what the pack starts on.

## Launcher
- **RAM**: 8 GB works well for this pack. 6 GB is about the minimum, and going much higher than 10 to 12 GB usually doesn't help and can cause stutters.
- **Use dedicated GPU**: turn it on if you have a laptop with two graphics cards, otherwise Minecraft might run on the weak one.
- **Performance mode**: the launcher has a performance mode for weaker PCs. Try it if the game struggles.

## Video (Options, then Video Settings)
- **FOV**: the pack starts at Normal (70). A lot of people prefer 90, which shows more of what's around you. Change it under Options.
- **Render distance**: starts at 8 chunks. 10 to 12 looks a lot better if your PC can take it. Farsight lets you go up to 64 on your side even if the server keeps its own distance lower.
- **Simulation distance**: starts at 8. Keep it low; it affects how far away farms and mobs keep running, and costs a lot of performance.
- **Max FPS** is 260 with VSync off. Turn VSync on if you see screen tearing.
- **Particles**: set to All. Drop it to Decreased if big fights or magic effects make your FPS dip. Magic spells throw out a lot of particles.
- **Clouds** are off by default. **GUI scale** starts at 3.
- Embeddium adds extra pages to Video Settings (quality, performance, advanced). The defaults are fine; entity culling and other speedups are already on.

## Shaders (Oculus)
- Shaders are turned on but no pack is selected, so you won't see any until you pick one. Press `O` to open the shader list (or Options, Video Settings, Shader Packs), and `K` switches shaders on and off.
- Five packs come with the game: **Complementary Unbound**, **BSL**, **Solas**, **Bliss** and **Sildur's Vibrant (Extreme-VL)**. Complementary and BSL are the easiest on most PCs. Bliss, Solas and Sildur's Extreme-VL are heavier.
- Each shader pack has its own settings screen (the button next to it in the list) where you can lower shadows and clouds for more FPS.

## Distant Horizons (far terrain)
- It's installed but **off by default**. Turn rendering on in its settings (the Distant Horizons button in Video Settings) to see terrain far past your normal render distance.
- It's set to a 256 chunk distance, which is very heavy. Lower it to 64 to 128 if your PC can't keep up.
- It also builds far terrain in the background, which uses CPU while you play. Shaders plus Distant Horizons together is the heaviest setup you can run.

## Sound
- AmbientSounds, Sound Physics (echo and muffling) and Presence Footsteps are all on. If something is too loud, AmbientSounds and Presence Footsteps have their own settings (`F10` for footsteps), and the vanilla sound sliders still work.

## HUD and info
- Iron's Spells shows your mana bar (above the hunger bar) and spell bar all the time. You can move them in the Iron's Spells client config.
- Minimap settings: `F7`. Inventory HUD on or off: `I`. Mob health plaques: `Shift` + `J`. Jade block info: `Numpad 1`.
- Legendary Tooltips and Enchantment Descriptions are on, so rare items and enchanted books explain themselves when you hover them.

## Elytra (Do a Barrel Roll)
- It's on by default, with banking (tilting into turns) enabled. Thrust is turned off in its settings, so the `W` thrust key does nothing unless you enable thrust in the mod's config (Mods menu, Do a Barrel Roll, Config).
- `Shift` + `I` switches the whole flight mode off if you prefer vanilla elytra controls.

## Accessibility
- Toggle crouch and toggle sprint are off by default. Turn them on under Options, Controls if holding keys gets tiring. Auto-jump is off.

# What you can do in BSCraft
The main things the pack lets you do, grouped by type. It isn't a complete list, and each part has its own section further down.

## Exploring
- Explore dungeons, castles, ruins, towns, taverns and boss lairs from around 20 structure mods (When Dungeons Arise, Dungeons and Taverns, Trek, YUNG's, Moog's and more)
- Travel through 150+ new overworld biomes and new cave biomes
- Visit the Twilight Forest, a separate magic dimension with its own bosses and progression
- Find the six hidden Alex's Caves biomes by decoding cave tablets into maps
- Explore the rebuilt Nether and End, with new biomes, metals and structures
- Fly to the Moon, Mars, Mercury and Venus (Northstar)
- Build your own dimension (RFTools Dimensions)

## Fighting
- Fight 40+ bosses. Some live in lairs, others are summoned with rituals or items
- Collect weapons: 15 weapon types and about 40 unique weapons with abilities (Simply Swords), plus weapons from the boss and dimension mods
- Collect armor sets from about a dozen mods, and 44 magic accessories (Artifacts)
- Use ranged and magic gear: special bows, beam guns, staffs, scepters and summoning items
- Enchant with 37 extra enchantments (Ensorcellation), plus item-specific ones from other mods

## Magic
- Build your own spells out of glyphs (Ars Nouveau), or find, craft and level up well over 200 spells across 9 core schools plus 5 addon schools (Iron's Spells and its addons)
- Cast rituals that change the weather, grow forests, stop mob spawns or summon bosses
- Put Starbuncles, Wixies, Drygmies and other helpers to work moving items, crafting, brewing and farming
- Fight wizard bosses: the Dead King, Echo of Tyros, the Wilden Chimera, seven Archmages, and a copy of yourself
- Wear mage robes that trade toughness for mana and spell power, or Archmage armor that beats netherite
- Brew mana drinks, cook magic food, and tie magic into Create with spell turrets and Source machines

## Creatures
- Run into 150+ new animals and monsters across every dimension
- Tame pets and mounts, from eagles and elephants to dinosaurs and dragons
- Trade with the Deep Ones, an underwater mob group, for gear you can't craft

## Building and tech
- Build machines and factories with Create
- Make power, from water wheels up to nuclear reactors
- Run trains, and program computers and mining turtles
- Buff your base with RFTools fields (flight, regeneration, no hostile spawns)

## Getting around
- Planes, airships, physics ships you build yourself, submarines, trains, rockets, rideable mounts, and a free waystone network

## Everyday stuff
- Cook meals that give buffs, brew drinks, age wine, and farm around 60 crops
- Build and decorate with thousands of furniture pieces and block variants
- Carry storage and tools around in upgradeable backpacks
- Grow magic trees, transmute items, or turn an area into a different biome

## New in this update
- **Live in a village properly**: villagers are people now, so you can befriend them, hire them, marry one and raise a family. See Villagers.
- **Clear forests fast**: one log fells a whole tree, and dropped saplings replant themselves.
- **Travel from the map**: stand at any waystone, open the world map, and jump to any other one you've found.
- **Light your way hands-free**: wear a lantern on your belt.
- **Enchant on a conveyor**: with Create, experience becomes a fluid and enchanting becomes a production line.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Difficulty tiers
People often ask what to do first. There's no set order here.

This isn't a route, just a rough sense of how tough each part of the pack is and what you'll find at each level, so you know what you're walking into. Go anywhere, in any order. Only the Twilight Forest locks its own bosses behind each other, and plenty of players wander into a tier-4 cave on day two just to see what happens. Risky trips cost less than you might think: your items wait in a corpse when you die, and every chest has its own loot for you even if someone got there first.

If you want a suggested order anyway, reading the tiers from top to bottom is one. Treat it as a difficulty scale, not a checklist: skip ahead, double back, or ignore whole tiers you're not interested in.

### Tier 1: Overworld basics (wood to iron)
At this tier: near-vanilla gear. Create's Andesite Alloy machines, Farmer's Delight cooking, Sophisticated Backpacks and Waystones are all within reach from the start.

Loot at this tier: YUNG's dungeons, mineshafts and desert or jungle temples all carry Artifacts (20 to 35% per chest) and can roll a Simply Swords unique. Every Lootr chest is yours alone.

Magic at this tier: everyone gets an Ars Nouveau starter book on their first join, and Iron's scrolls and Arcane Essence turn up in normal chests. Wandering Magician robes (leather armor plus Arcane Essence) are the first mana armor. Mangrove Huts and Wilden Dens are fairly common.

Threats: Rotten Creatures zombie variants. Watch for Skreechers (they can summon a Warden) and Spawn's land-walking Drowned at night.

### Tier 2: Brass age and first bosses
At this tier: Brass (heated mixer), steam engines, ore doubling. Make an Iron Simply Swords weapon: an iron katana already hits for 6 at 2.0 speed. Enter the Twilight Forest by throwing a diamond into a flower-ringed pool.

Gear found here: Naga armor, Ironwood/Steeleaf, a Lich scepter, Charm of Life. From Magnetic Caves: Galena Gauntlet and Resistor Shield.

Magic at this tier: Ars Source Gems, Source Jars and the Apprentice spell book; Iron's Scroll Forge, Arcane Cloth and Wizard or school robes. Cade, the Frost Archmage (325 HP), is the easiest Adam's Ars Plus boss.

Threats: Naga (120 HP) then Lich (100). Ferrous Wroughtnaut (40 HP, but hits for 30, only vulnerable after its slam) gives the unbreakable 9-damage Axe of a Thousand Metals. Skor the Yeti (250 HP) gives the Ice Gauntlet.

### Tier 3: Diamond and the Nether
At this tier: Diamond Simply Swords weapons. BetterNether ores: Cincinnasite to Nether Ruby to Flaming Ruby (netherite-level, 0.3 knockback res). Raid Bastion treasure rooms (65% Artifact chance) and Bygone Nether catacombs.

Gear found here: Minoshroom's guaranteed Diamond Minotaur Axe, the Knight Phantom chest (Knightmetal plus Phantom armor), Fiery gear from Hydra blood, Yeti armor, the Three-Bolt suit.

Magic at this tier: the Dead King (500 HP) in the Catacombs, for the Blood Staff or Necronomicon. The Wilden Chimera (1,000 HP) drops the Wilden Tribute for the Archmage spell book. Ryan (500 HP, in the Nether), Nick (900) and Cam (925) give the mid-tier Archmage armor. Iron's Arcane Anvil and Upgrade Orbs open up.

Threats: Minoshroom, Hydra (360 HP, bring Fire Resistance), Knight Phantoms (6 times 35 HP), Alpha Yeti (200). Captain Cornelia (200 HP, Ice Maze). Kraken (500), Sirok (150), Ashlord (250).

### Tier 4: Netherite and legendaries
At this tier: netherite-upgrade your Simply Swords weapon, then go Runic (+1 damage and a random power). Hunt uniques in every non-village chest. Decode Cave Tablets to find the Toxic Caves and Forlorn Hollows.

Gear found here: Raygun, Hazmat suit, Dreadbow, Hood + Cloak of Darkness, the Abyssal set (from Mother of the Maze), Divider, Whisper of the Abyss, and Ur-Ghast and Snow Queen drops (Triple Bow).

Magic at this tier: Echo of Tyros (1,000 HP) in the Nether Citadel, for the Divine Soulshard and the Decrepit Scythe (which upgrades to Hellrazor). Matt, the Holy Archmage (1,225 HP). The Ancient Codex, Dread Robes and Boreal Blade.

Threats: Luxtructosaurus (600 HP, gives the Extinction Spear), Graveyard Lich (400 + 200 HP), Helvar (250, key-gated Nether arena), Ur-Ghast (250), Snow Queen (200), Forsaken (250), Hullbreaker (400).

### Tier 5: The End
At this tier: fight the True Ending dragon (about 300 HP, dives, laser, phantom-guarded crystals). BetterEnd: Thallasium to Terminite to Aeternium. End Cities for the Elytra (Lootr gives every player their own from the same ship). The Eternal Portal gives you a second Overworld-End link.

Gear found here: the Aeternium set plus Armored Elytra, Tarantula Hawk Elytra, Chorus Totem or Crystal Heart artifacts (End City, 30%), and a 50% Simply Swords roll from the dragon.

Magic at this tier: Adam, the Void Archmage (2,000 HP), lives in the Void Fortress on the outer End islands and drops the Void Souls for the strongest armor in the pack. Josh (1,800 HP) is summoned with the Mage Summon ritual and a Manipulation Essence. The Dark Doppelganger (6,000 HP) can only be summoned in the End.

Threats: Ender Dragon, then Void Worm (160 HP, summoned by throwing a Mysterious Worm into the End void; hit the head or it splits). Its drops craft the Dimensional Carver. You can hatch the dragon egg with Dragon Mounts.

### Tier 6: Endgame engineering and space
At this tier: Create Nuclear or Bigger Reactors for huge power. RFTools Environmental Controller (Flight, Regen and Peaceful fields) and custom RFTools Dimensions. Northstar rockets: Moon to Mars to Mercury or Venus.

Gear found here: the Martian Steel Space Suit (heat-resistant), Transmutation Table, a Warship aircraft, and a tamed Tremorzilla (hatch its egg with a nuclear blast).

Threats: Tremorzilla (500 HP, 30 damage; tame it, don't fight it), the RFTools legendary Blob (5,000 HP), and whatever your nukes wake up.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Ways people play
A few of the directions the pack supports end to end. They aren't classes or quests, just examples. Mix them, drop them, or invent something nobody's thought of.

### Explorer
Structures, caves, dimensions
1. Loot YUNG's and Moog's structures, taverns, When Dungeons Arise, and pick up every wild waystone on the way
2. Decode Cave Tablets into Cave Maps (all 6 Alex's Caves biomes)
3. Twilight Forest landmarks
4. Bastions, Ancient Cities, Mansions
5. BetterEnd biomes + Eternal Portal
6. RFTools custom worlds

### Boss hunter
Kill everything with a health bar
1. Wroughtnaut, Skor, Umvuthi
2. Twilight chain: Naga to Lich to Hydra / Knights / Yeti
3. Cornelia, Kraken, Sirok, Ashlord
4. Luxtructosaurus, Graveyard Lich, Helvar, the Dead King
5. Ender Dragon (True Ending), Void Worm, the Adam's Ars Plus Archmages
6. Snow Queen, Ur-Ghast, then Troll Caves

### Mage
Spells, rituals, familiars
1. Ars starter book, Source Gems, a Scribe's Table
2. Iron's scrolls, Inscription Table, Scroll Forge
3. Starbuncles, Wixies and rituals running the base
4. Wilden Chimera for the Archmage book, the Dead King and Echo of Tyros for Iron's best gear
5. Adam's Ars Plus Archmages, from Cade up to Adam
6. The Dark Doppelganger in the End

### Homesteader
Food, drink, farms, building
1. Farmer's Delight cooking (Comfort + Nourishment)
2. Croptopia's ~60 crops and 30 fruit trees
3. Vinery wines (26 effects, aging makes them stronger)
4. Brewin' & Chewin' kegs, Drink Beer
5. Create-automated kitchen (Slice & Dice)
6. Macaw's, Handcrafted, Chipped, Dawn of Time builds, lit with Curious Lanterns
7. A forest that replants itself (FallingTree and Saplanting)

### Engineer
Create, trains, reactors, automation
1. Andesite to Brass to Precision Mechanism
2. A Stirling engine on a coal burner, then steam engines, then diesel
3. Trains with Steam 'n' Rails conductors
4. Alternator (SU to FE) to RFTools machines
5. Create Nuclear or Bigger Reactors
6. An enchanting line on liquid experience, then ComputerCraft factory control

### Village keeper
Villagers, families, a town of your own
1. Befriend villagers with gifts and conversation, and learn who trades what
2. Hire a few and give them chores: farming, fishing, chopping, cooking
3. A Bouquet, then rings from a Jeweler Workbench, and marry someone
4. Raise children, who grow up and work alongside you
5. Run the village with the Blueprint: guards, the marriage limit, its waystone
6. Keep a Staff of Life for the day someone you care about doesn't make it

### Aviator and captain
Planes, airships, subs, dragons
1. Biplane (Create mechanical crafting)
2. Airship to Cargo Airship to Warship
3. VS2 ships with Clockwork / Propulsion
4. Alex's Caves Submarine + torpedoes
5. Tamed Bald Eagle, Gum Worm, Atlatitan rides
6. Hatch and raise a dragon

### Astronaut
Northstar space program
1. Create base + Oxygen Separator
2. Jet Engine, Rocket Station, Controls
3. Iron Space Suit to the Moon (titanium, lunar sapphire)
4. Interplanetary Navigator to Mars
5. Martian Steel suit to Mercury / Venus
6. Liquid hydrogen fuel (2x burn time)

# Biomes
BSCraft piles on well over 150 new biomes on top of vanilla, spread across half a dozen mods. Oh The Biomes We've Gone and Regions Unexplored cover most of the overworld, Nyctophobia adds a few rare spooky variants, YUNG's Cave Biomes adds new cave types, and BetterNether and BetterEnd rebuild the Nether and the End from the ground up. A couple of magic mods and the extra dimensions stack their own biomes on top of that. If you're hunting for a specific one, hold Nature's Compass and right click: it searches outward from you and points to the nearest match, for any biome on this list.

## Overworld

### Oh The Biomes We've Gone
Oh The Biomes We've Gone (BWG) adds 54 overworld biomes. All of them are enabled except Eroded Borealis, which is turned off in this pack's config.

By climate:
- **Cold/snowy**: Aspen Boreal, Canadian Shield, Crimson Tundra, Eroded Borealis (disabled), Frosted Coniferous Forest, Frosted Taiga, Howling Peaks, Shattered Glacier, Skyris Vale
- **Temperate forest/grassland**: Black Forest, Cika Woods, Coconino Meadow, Coniferous Forest, Ebony Woods, Enchanted Tangle, Forgotten Forest, Ironwood Gour, Maple Taiga, Overgrowth Woodlands, Prairie, Sakura Grove, Temperate Grove, Weeping Witch Forest, Zelkova Forest, Rose Fields, Allium Shrubland, Amaranth Grassland
- **Jungle/tropical/swamp**: Bayou, Cypress Swamplands, Cypress Wetlands, Fragment Jungle, Jacaranda Jungle, Lush Stacks, Overgrowth Woodlands, Tropical Rainforest, White Mangrove Marshes, Orchard, Pumpkin Valley
- **Savanna/mediterranean**: Araucaria Savanna, Baobab Savanna, Firecracker Chaparral, Crag Gardens
- **Desert/badlands**: Atacama Outback, Dead Sea, Mojave Desert, Red Rock Peaks, Red Rock Valley, Rugged Badlands, Sierra Badlands, Windswept Desert
- **Coast/misc**: Basalt Barrera, Dacite Ridges, Dacite Shore, Rainbow Beach

Notable ones:
- **Sakura Grove**: pink cherry trees, all vanilla mob spawns. Good building wood and a nice place to look at.
- **Crag Gardens**: rocky, garden-like terrain, with glow squid spawning underwater.
- **Dead Sea**: hot salt flat and desert-coast mix. Spawns drowned instead of zombies.
- **Skyris Vale**: cold plains, home to its own Skyris village structure. Monster list is trimmed down to spider, skeleton, slime, enderman and witch, no zombies or creepers.
- **Howling Peaks**: cold mountains, extra stray (frozen skeleton) spawns plus glow squid underground, goats, foxes and wolves on the surface.
- **Enchanted Tangle**: dense magical-looking jungle, double spawn weight for ocelot, parrot and panda, glow squid underground.
- **Weeping Witch Forest**: extra-weighted witch and cave spider spawns, and its own Salem witch village structure. Probably the best BWG biome for witch farming outside an actual hut.
- **Lush Stacks**: coastal, spawns the custom Man O' War mob (a hostile jellyfish) plus drowned, dolphins and tropical fish.
- **Allium Shrubland**: the only biome that spawns the custom Oddion mob.
- **Crimson Tundra**: snowy badlands mix, polar bear and stray spawns.

### Regions Unexplored
Regions Unexplored (RU) adds 71 biomes: 63 in the overworld, 5 overworld cave biomes, and 5 in the Nether. All 71 are enabled.

By climate (overworld):
- **Cold/taiga**: Boreal Taiga, Cold Boreal Taiga, Frozen Pine Taiga, Frozen Tundra, Golden Boreal Taiga, Icy Heights, Blackwood Taiga, Pine Taiga, Pine Slopes, Silver Birch Forest, Chalk Cliffs, Spires
- **Temperate forest**: Alpha Grove, Ashen Woodland, Autumnal Maple Forest, Cold Deciduous Forest, Deciduous Forest, Magnolia Woodland, Maple Forest, Redwoods, Sparse Redwoods, Temperate Grove, Willow Forest, Hyacinth Deeps
- **Grass/farm/flower**: Barley Fields, Clover Plains, Flower Fields, Grassland, Highland Fields, Poppy Fields, Prairie, Pumpkin Fields, Rocky Meadow, Steppe, Mountains, Towering Cliffs, Mauve Hills, Arid Mountains
- **Wet/swamp**: Bayou, Old Growth Bayou, Fen, Fungal Fen, Marsh, Muddy River, Cold River, Tropical River
- **Jungle/tropical**: Rainforest, Sparse Rainforest, Eucalyptus Forest, Bamboo Forest, Tropics
- **Savanna/orchard**: Baobab Savanna, Orchard
- **Desert/dry**: Joshua Desert, Saguaro Desert, Outback, Dry Bushland
- **Coast**: Grassy Beach, Gravel Beach, Rocky Reef

Overworld caves (5): Ancient Delta, Bioshroom Caves, Prismachasm, Redstone Caves, Scorching Caves.
Nether (5): Blackstone Basin, Glistering Meadow, Infernal Holt, Mycotoxic Undergrowth, Redstone Abyss.

Woodland Mansions generate in Blackwood Taiga and Redwoods, Jungle Temples in Rainforest, Sparse Rainforest, Eucalyptus Forest and Bamboo Forest, and Desert Pyramids in Joshua Desert, Saguaro Desert and Outback.

Notable ones:
- **Redstone Caves**: redstone and copper themed, with Redstone Bud and Redstone Bulb light blocks. Mobs are mostly vanilla cave spawns.
- **Prismachasm**: has its own Prismarite Cluster and Prismaglass blocks, like an amethyst geode.
- **Bioshroom Caves and Ancient Delta**: both spawn axolotls and tropical fish underground, lush-cave style.
- **Blackstone Basin** (Nether): spawns wither skeletons alongside striders, zombified piglins and endermen. Probably the best Nether biome here for wither skulls outside a fortress.
- **Glistering Meadow** (Nether): spawns ghasts and hoglins, plus its own Glistering Bloom, Fern, Ivy, Nylium, Sprout and Wart blocks.
- **Infernal Holt and Mycotoxic Undergrowth** (Nether): piglin, hoglin and strider spawns. Mycotoxic Undergrowth also has its own Mycotoxic Daisy, Grass, Moss and Mushroom blocks. These two plus Redstone Abyss also get BetterNether's Firefly, Hydrogen Jellyfish and Naga mixed into their spawns.
- **Chalk Cliffs**: its own Chalk building blocks (bricks, pillar, stairs, slab), sheep spawns.
- **Hyacinth Deeps**: wetland forest, drowned and fish spawns, plus Hyacinth Bloom, Flowers and Lamp blocks.
- **Spires**: the coldest RU biome, polar bears, strays and rabbits.

### Nyctophobia
Nyctophobia adds 5 rare, spooky biomes, and they don't add new space so much as replace a vanilla biome slot. All 5 are enabled.

- Ancient Dead Coral Reef replaces Beach.
- Haunted Forest replaces Windswept Forest (fog density 0.35, between y63 and y148).
- Haunted Lakes replaces Swamp (fog density 0.30, between y63 and y128).
- Eroded Haunted Forest replaces Meadow (fog density 0.35, between y93 and y160).
- Deep Dark Forest replaces Dark Forest.

They're low weight, so you won't run into them often. They read as dark-forest and swamp reskins with heavier fog and a spookier palette rather than biomes with their own footprint.

### Ecologics
Ecologics doesn't add any biomes of its own. It just drops its custom crops (dragonfruit, blueberries, starfruit and others) into existing Beach, Desert, Snowy, Plains and Lush Caves biomes.

## Magic biomes
Ars Nouveau and Ars Zero each add a biome on top of the overworld.

- **Archwood Forest** (Ars Nouveau): needs TerraBlender to generate, which this pack has, and it's rare (spawn weight 2). Extra gold ore, naturally lit magic lights, and much higher spawn rates for Starbuncle, Whirlisprig and Drygmy, plus Archwood Treants. Archwood trees come in 4 colors (Blazing/red, Cascading/blue, Flourishing/green, Vexing/purple) and can rarely show up in any biome, but they cluster here. The Source Berry Bush (a mana food and potion ingredient) grows in Taiga and Archwood Forest.
- **Ars Elemental** can add 4 single-color Archwood forests (Blazing, Cascading, Flashing and Flourishing), but they're switched off in this pack's config. Its gold Flashing Archwood tree still shows up mixed into normal Archwood Forests.
- **Ars Zero's Blight Forest** is the corrupted, undead version of Archwood Forest: dead archwood, blighted soil, blight veins, and entrances down into the Necropolis, Ars Zero's dungeon full of undead spellcasters, Bone Chests and Ossuary Beacons. The biome itself doesn't spawn anything special; the danger is in the Necropolis underneath.

## Caves
**YUNG's Cave Biomes** adds 2 new cave biomes and one tweak to a vanilla one:
- **Frosted Caves**: cold cave biome, source of Icicle, Frost Lily and Enchanted Ice (mine it for Frost potions), plus Creeping Ice. Spawns the custom Ice Cube mob and applies a Frost effect that slows you and deals damage over time.
- **Lost Caves**: hot, dry sandstone cave biome, source of Ancient Sand and Sandstone (with its own Ancient armor trim), Marble, Travertine and edible Prickly Peach Cactus. Spawns the custom Sand Snapper mob, and gets periodic Sandstorms that slow you and narrow your view.
- Dripstone Caves just get a cosmetic and spawn tweak, not a full biome swap.

**Alex's Caves** has its own 6 hidden cave biomes, and none of them are disabled. Finding them works differently from everything else here (Cave Tablets, the Codex, then a map) so see the Alex's Caves section for the full unlock chain.

## The Nether
BetterNether adds 22 biomes, all present: Bone Reef, Crimson Glowing Woods, Crimson Pinewood, Flooded Deltas, Gravel Desert, Magma Land, Nether Grasslands, Nether Jungle, Nether Mushroom Forest, Nether Mushroom Forest Edge, Nether Swampland, Nether Swampland Terraces, Old Fungiwoods, Old Swampland, Old Warped Woods, Poor Nether Grasslands, Soul Plain, Sulfuric Bone Reef, Upside Down Forest, Upside Down Cleared Forest, Wart Forest, Wart Forest Edge.

Every single one of these shares the same custom mob spawn pool: Firefly, Hydrogen Jellyfish, Flying Pig, Jungle Skeleton, Skull and Naga. So which biome you're in comes down to terrain and wood type, not what spawns there. Nether Jungle and Old Fungiwoods are the spot for BetterNether's wood types, Magma Land and Gravel Desert are good for mining, Bone Reef and Sulfuric Bone Reef are bone terrain, and Soul Plain is the soul sand equivalent.

## The End
BetterEnd adds 25 biomes, all present, running its own custom End generator: the dragon still fights on the main island, the obsidian spikes and portal stay put, and End City has a 5% chance to fail generating.

Biomes: Amber Land, Blossoming Spires, Chorus Forest, Crystal Mountains, Dragon Graveyards, Dry Shrubland, Dust Wastelands, Empty Aurora Cave, Empty End Cave, Empty Smaragdant Cave, Foggy Mushroomland, Glowing Grasslands, Ice Starfield, Jade Cave, Lantern Woods, Lush Aurora Cave, Lush Smaragdant Cave, Megalake, Megalake Grove, Neon Oasis, Painted Mountains, Shadow Forest, Sulphur Springs, Umbra Valley, Umbrella Jungle.

Custom mobs spread across these: Cubozoa, Ender Dragonfly, End Fish, End Slime, Shadow Walker, Silk Moth.
- **Shadow Forest** is the only biome that spawns Shadow Walker, a dark and dangerous End forest.
- **Foggy Mushroomland, Megalake and Megalake Grove** get the full aquatic set: End Fish, Cubozoa, End Slime and Ender Dragonfly.
- **Blossoming Spires** is the only biome that spawns Silk Moth.
- **Amber Land and Chorus Forest** spawn End Slime.
- **Sulphur Springs** spawns Cubozoa and End Fish, geothermal terrain.
- The rest (Crystal Mountains, Dust Wastelands, Ice Starfield, Jade Cave and others) don't spawn anything beyond vanilla endermen. They're mostly resource and scenery biomes: crystal, glowing plants, ice, jade.

## Twilight Forest and the planets
- The Twilight Forest is its own dimension with 21 biomes and boss-gated progression. See the Twilight Forest section for the full rundown.
- Northstar adds 23 biomes across the Moon, Mars, Mercury and Venus. See the space section for details.

## Finding a specific biome
Nature's Compass covers everything above. Hold it, right click, and it reads directly from the biome registry, so it works on every modded biome in this list, not just vanilla ones. It won't help with Alex's Caves though. Those 6 biomes are hand placed instead of generated the normal way, so the compass can't point to them until you've already found and mapped them through the Cave Tablet and Codex loop. Once a cave biome is on your map, you can navigate to it like normal.

Outside of that one exception, if you're after a particular tree, ore or mob tied to a biome on this list, Nature's Compass is the fastest way to get there.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Structures and best loot

About 20 structure mods fill every dimension. Counted from the pack files, they can generate around 800 different structures from 455 spawn sets, so you are rarely more than a few hundred blocks from something built. Nearly all of them use chest loot tables, which means almost every one can roll a Simply Swords unique (0.1% per roll) and, for the vanilla types, an Artifact. YUNG's redesigns keep the vanilla loot tables, and Repurposed Structures copies modded loot into its biome variants. Village houses are the one exception: they never drop uniques.

## Best loot sources, ranked

1. **Mimic** (a chest that attacks): found anywhere, and underground campsites are 30% mimics. Top loot is a guaranteed random Artifact. Danger low to medium.
2. **Ender Dragon kill** (the End): top loot is 50% Simply Swords loot, the egg, and End access. Danger very high.
3. **Bastion treasure room** (Nether): top loot is a 65% Artifact (Crystal Heart, Antidote, Fire Gauntlet, Spikes, and more), netherite, and a 45% Magnetic Cave tablet. Danger high (Brutes).
4. **Ancient City** (Deep Dark, the Dungeons & Taverns overhaul): top loot is Artifacts, Swift Sneak books, echo shards, and uniques. Danger very high (Warden).
5. **Woodland Mansion** (Dark Forest): top loot is a 40% Artifact (Chorus Totem), a 75% Forlorn Hollows tablet, and totems. Danger high.
6. **End City plus Moog's End** (the End): top loot is Elytra, a 30% Artifact (Crystal Heart, Cloud in a Bottle), and enchanted diamond gear. Danger medium to high.
7. **When Dungeons Arise dungeons** (Overworld, everywhere): top loot is huge chest counts of enchanted books, uniques, and rare gear. Danger medium to high.
8. **Citadel Vault** (Iron's Spells, Nether): top loot is diamonds, Pyrium, Mithril Scrap, netherite and ancient debris, scrolls, runes and curios. It's the rarest magic structure. Danger high (Echo of Tyros and Keepers).
9. **Dead King's Vault** (Iron's Spells, Catacombs): top loot is golden apples, a small chance at an enchanted golden apple, Arcane Ingots, scrolls, runes and curios. Danger high.
10. **Twilight Forest landmarks**: top loot is boss chests, Trader Tokens (30% of chests), and charms. Danger varies.
11. **Aquamirae Frozen Chest** (Ice Maze, needs the Frozen Key): top loot is a Rune of the Storm, a 20% Dagger of Greed, an enchanted golden apple, and Astral Dust. Danger high.
12. **Underwater Ruins / Ocean Monument** (Ocean): top loot is a 45% Artifact (the highest curated odds) and a 40% Abyssal tablet. Danger medium.
13. **Stronghold** (YUNG's, Underground): top loot is a 30% Artifact (Power Glove, Superstitious Hat). Danger medium to high.
14. **Jungle Temple / Desert Pyramid** (Jungle/Desert): top loot is a 35%/20% Artifact and a 50% Toxic Caves tablet. Danger low to medium.
15. **Witch Hut** (YUNG's, Swamp): top loot is a 90% Candy Cavity tablet. Danger low.
16. **Wither kill** (anywhere): top loot is a Nether Star (Transmutation Table, Beacon) and 5% Simply Swords loot. Danger high.

## Structure mods and what they add
- **When Dungeons Arise** (39 structures): big dungeons with lots of mobs and chests. Includes the Illager Fort, Illager Galley and Illager Corsair ships, Illager Windmill, Bandit Towers and Bandit Village, the Heavenly Challenger, Conqueror and Rider sky structures, Keep Kayra, Shiraz Palace, the Plague Asylum, the Coliseum, the Mechanical Nest, the Foundry, the Scorched Mines and Mining System, the Mushroom Mines and Mushroom Village, Thornborn Towers, Typhon, the Undead Pirate Ship, a Monastery, an Aviary, a Lighthouse, a Small Blimp and more.
- **Dungeons and Taverns** (58): taverns in most biomes, crypts (Creeping Crypt, Undead Crypt), a Donjon, an Illager Manor and Illager Hideout, a Stray Fort, bunkers, ruined towns and firewatch towers. In the Nether it adds the Nether Keep, a Nether Port, piglin camps and outstations, and skeleton towers. It also overhauls the Ancient City.
- **Trek** (125): a big mix of castles, forts, pillager mansions and bases, elven trees, hobbit holes, haunted houses, floating farms, buried villages, ice forts, oases and a lot more.
- **Moog's Voyager** (130) and **Moog's Soaring** (35): lots of smaller vanilla-style builds, from wells, camps and carts to cathedrals and castle ruins, plus sky builds like floating islands, towers and a volcano.
- **Moog's Temples** (6): reworked badlands, desert, jungle, ocean and Nether temples, and a stronghold.
- **Moog's Nether** (44) and **Moog's End** (24): Nether towers, shrines, giant skulls and wells. End spires, the Phantom Citadel, crashed Mega Ships and more.
- **Towns and Towers** (59): biome and culture versions of villages and pillager outposts (Tudor, Mediterranean, Iberian, Oriental, Swedish and others).
- **Tidal Towns**: ocean villages out in deep water, with their own farms and blacksmith. **No More Villages** switches off the plain vanilla villages, and Towns and Towers generates its own in their place.
- **Epic Villages** ships only loot tables and tags in this pack, with no structures of its own, and vanilla villages are switched off, so it has nothing to attach to. It isn't doing anything at the moment.
- **Repurposed Structures** (101): biome versions of vanilla structures, like Nether and End ancient cities, jungle fortresses, desert and snowy mansions, themed mineshafts, outposts, pyramids and monuments. It copies modded loot into every variant.
- **YUNG's** (9 mods): redesigned dungeons, mineshafts, strongholds, Nether fortresses, ocean monuments, desert and jungle temples, witch huts and the End island, plus natural bridges. Same loot tables as vanilla, a lot more rooms.
- **Forgotten Ruins** (8): ruins and libraries guarded by golems.
- **The Graveyard** (17): graveyards of different sizes, crypts, haunted houses and the Lich Prison.
- **Bygone Nether**: catacombs, a citadel and a piglin manor in the Nether.
- **BetterNether** and **BetterEnd**: Nether cities, pyramids, temples and ghast hives. End villages and the Eternal Portal.
- **Boss lairs**: Bosses'Rise (Dragon Tower, Kraken Ship, Sandworm Nest, Yeti Hideout, Underworld Arena), Mowzie's Mobs (Umvuthana Grove, the Wroughtnaut's chamber), Aquamirae (the Ice Maze with its ships, shelters and outposts) and Alex's Caves (Underground Cabins, Abyssal Ruins, Gingerbread Town, Licowitch Tower, the volcano).
- **Magic structures**: Iron's Spells adds the Catacombs (the Dead King), the Citadel and Ancient Battleground in the Nether (Echo of Tyros), Pyromancer and Mountain Towers, the Evoker Fort, Mangrove Huts, Ice Spider Dens, the Impaled Icebreaker, and Priest Houses inside Repurposed Structures villages. Adam's Ars Plus adds six Archmage dungeons: the Frozen Library (snowy biomes), Infernal Prison (Nether Wastes), Overgrown Barracks (Deep Dark), Nimbostratic Ruins (mountains), Holy Mausoleum (Soul Sand Valley) and Void Fortress (outer End islands). Ars Nouveau has Wilden Dens, Ars Additions the Arcane Library, Nexus Tower and Ruined Warp Portals, Ars Zero the Necropolis, Magic From The East the Onmyoji Shrine and Taoist House, Wind's Spellbooks the Windmill, and Geomancy Plus the Geomancer Tower.

## How often you run into them
Spacing is the average number of chunks between attempts, taken from each mod's own spawn settings. Smaller means more common.
- **Mineshafts**: every chunk. The densest thing in the pack, and YUNG's version replaces the vanilla one.
- **Repurposed Structures** (101 structures): anywhere from every chunk to every 400, depending on the variant.
- **Moog's Voyager** (130): every 12 to 112 chunks, mostly Overworld.
- **Trek** (125): every 16 to 150 chunks.
- **Towns and Towers** villages (59): every 32 to 48 chunks.
- **Dungeons and Taverns** (58): every 20 to 200 chunks.
- **Moog's Nether** (44): every 22 to 115 chunks. **Moog's End** (24): every 10 to 88.
- **When Dungeons Arise** (39): every 35 to 60 chunks.
- **The Graveyard** (17): every 12 to 120 chunks.
- **Iron's Spells** structures (5): every 72 to 85 chunks, the rarest of the magic builds.

## What this pack changes about vanilla structures
- **YUNG's mods replace their vanilla counterpart and switch the original off**: mineshafts, Nether fortresses, jungle temples, ocean monuments, witch huts and desert pyramids. You will never see the plain versions.
- **Desert temples give you Mining Fatigue** the whole time you are inside one you have not cleared. It lifts when you deal with the tomb at the end. This is on in this pack.
- **Wither skeletons from Better Dungeons spawners drop skulls**, and blazes from spawners drop rods, so those dungeons are a real farm. Small Nether dungeons are switched off.
- **The End has no vanilla obsidian spawn platform**, because the End island is rebuilt.
- **Vanilla villages are off entirely** (No More Villages). What you find are Towns and Towers villages, which is why the update put waystone plazas in the Biomes We've Gone villages rather than the vanilla ones.

## Overlaps worth knowing
- **Three mods claim the stronghold**: YUNG's Better Strongholds, Repurposed Structures and Trek all write the tag the Eye of Ender follows. Whichever loads last wins, so what you walk into can differ from what a wiki shows.
- **Mineshafts come from two places**: YUNG's replaces the vanilla one, Repurposed Structures adds its own biome variants on top.
- Five mods write the village biome tags. Only one ends up in charge, and that is why village style can look inconsistent between saves.

## Which mods actually hold the top-tier loot
Counted from each mod's chest tables: how many of them can roll netherite, an enchanted golden apple, a totem, an Elytra or a nether star.
- **Repurposed Structures**: 78 tables. Far more shots at good loot than anything else, spread across every biome variant.
- **When Dungeons Arise**: 30 tables, concentrated in the big dungeons.
- **Dungeons and Taverns**: 10, including the illager manor and the Nether fort.
- **BetterNether** (6) and **The Graveyard** (6).
- **Iron's Spells** (4): the catacombs and battleground chests.
- **Moog's Nether** (3) and **Northstar** bases (3).
- **BetterEnd** (2), and one of those is the End village Elytra.

## Waystones in the world
- Waystones generate on their own about every 24 chunks, in every surface biome, as one of three builds: a blossom arch, a moonlight gazebo or a fairy ring. Each has lanterns and a campfire, so the smoke column gives them away from a distance.
- The stone matches the biome: plain, mossy in wet and green biomes, sandy in deserts, snowy in the cold. The Nether and the End get a plinth instead.
- Villages carry their own waystone, usually near the middle.
- They're free to use once you've found them, and you can travel to them from the world map. See Quality of life for how that works.

## Good to know
- Lootr: every chest is per-player. You can all loot the same dungeon, and each roll is fixed once you open it. So places other players already explored still have loot waiting for you. The Quality of life section explains how it works.
- FixMySpawnR: dungeon spawners shut off after a while. A redstone signal keeps a mob-farm spawner running.
- Finding structures: Xaero's map shows explored structures, cartographers sell explorer and cabin maps, Nature's Compass finds biomes (so you can pick the biome a structure spawns in), Cave Maps find Alex's Caves biomes, and TF Magic Maps show Twilight landmarks.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# The Nether and the End

Both dimensions are rebuilt from scratch in this pack.

## The Nether

BetterNether adds biomes (Nether Jungle, Rusty Pit, Old Fungus, Obsidian Groves, grand caves, and more), mobs, and two metal lines. Bygone Nether adds ancient structures, catacombs and new mobs. YUNG's Better Fortresses and Moog's Nether Structures add more loot rooms. Bastion treasure rooms give the best vanilla-structure odds for Artifacts, at 65%.

- **Cincinnasite** (iron tier): 17 armor, 1.0 toughness, 0.05 knockback resistance. First thing you can make in the Nether, and its tools get +0.2 speed. A diamond-template upgrade raises the tools to diamond level.
- **Nether Ruby** (diamond tier): 18 armor, 1.4 toughness, 0.2 knockback resistance. Tools have 2,561 durability.
- **Flaming Ruby** (netherite tier, a smithing upgrade): 20 armor, 2.6 toughness, 0.3 knockback resistance. Tools have 2,861 durability, +0.4 speed, and enchantability 32.
- **Gilded Netherite** (netherite tier): 20 armor, 3.0 toughness, 0.1 knockback resistance. Netherite plus gold armor, so piglins stay neutral until the gold wears off.
- **Wither Skeleton Knight** (Bygone Nether): 60 HP. An armored mini-boss in the catacombs, knock its armor off first.
- **Corpor** (Bygone Nether): 60 HP, hits for 4 damage.
- **Wraither / Wex** (Bygone Nether): 25 and 14 HP. Fliers and adds.
- **Hydrogen Jellyfish** (BetterNether): 0.5 HP. Explodes for about 20 damage, don't melee it.
- **Naga** (BetterNether): 10 HP. A projectile spitter found in the Nether Jungle.
- **Flying Pig / Wither Skeleton Horse** (BetterNether/Bygone Nether): 6 HP. Rideable mounts.

- **Bartering Station**: put gold in it and nearby piglins barter with it on their own, dropping the results into the station. An easy way to farm piglin trades.

## The End

YUNG's Better End Island redesigns the dragon arena, and True Ending overhauls the fight (see the Bosses section). Beyond the island, BetterEnd adds about a dozen biomes (Crystal Mountains, Megalake, Shadow Forest, Amber Land, Lantern Woods, and more), new ores, and the Eternal Portal. Moog's End Structures scatter loot stops across the islands.

- **Thallasium** (iron mining level): 12 armor, 0 toughness, 0 knockback resistance, sword does 5.5 damage. Tools have 320 durability.
- **Terminite** (diamond mining level): 19 armor, 1.0 toughness, 0.05 knockback resistance, sword does 7 damage. Tools have 1,230 durability, forged on the Terminite Anvil.
- **Crystalite**: 20 armor, 1.2 toughness, 0.1 knockback resistance. Armor only, made by infusing Terminite pieces with Crystal Shards and Amber. Gives Haste, Speed, blindness immunity, and a set-bonus Regen.
- **Aeternium** (mining level 5, above netherite): 24 armor, 3.5 toughness, 0.2 knockback resistance, sword does 8.5 damage. The best raw armor in the pack, tools have 2,196 durability, and it includes the Armored Elytra.

### Eternal Portal: a second way between the Overworld and the End

1. Find the unlit Eternal Portal structure in the End, a ring of runed flavolite with 6 pedestals.
2. Craft an Eternal Crystal with an Infusion Ritual: an End Crystal on the center pedestal, with 4 Crystal Shards (from Crystal Mountains) and 4 Ender Shards around it. It takes 12.5 seconds.
3. Put a crystal on each of the 6 pedestals. The portal lights up and links to the matching X/Z coordinates in the Overworld.

- Hammers (every material, including netherite at 10 damage and Aeternium at 11.5) mine in an area around where you hit.
- End mobs: Shadow Walker (an invisible ambusher in the Shadow Forest), Dragonflies, End Fish, Cubozoa, Silk Moths, End Slimes.
- Void Worm: throw a Mysterious Worm into the void past the island (see the Bosses section).
- Dragon Mounts: dragon eggs don't spawn in chests in this pack. Use the dragon egg instead (a new one appears after each re-summoned dragon). Hatch it in a themed spot to change its breed: near lava for fire, underwater for water, snow for ice, and so on.

## Worth the trip
- **Nether cities** (BetterNether) have chest tables that can roll netherite, the best odds in the Nether outside bastions.
- **Bygone Nether's citadel** can roll netherite and enchanted golden apples, and its catacombs hide netherite too.
- **Moog's Nether Structures** adds 44 builds across the Nether, every 22 to 115 chunks, with treasure chests that can hold netherite and enchanted golden apples.
- **End villages** (BetterEnd) include a bonus chest that can hold an Elytra, which makes them worth checking even if you already have one from an End Ship.
- **Moog's End Structures** adds 24 builds scattered across the outer islands.

## The dragon fight here
True Ending rebuilds the Ender Dragon rather than just adding health.
- Some of its attacks go straight through a shield and skip the shield cooldown, so blocking is not a plan.
- It throws a heavier fireball, and phantoms fly in to guard it.
- End crystals matter more than in vanilla, and the fight has its own music and effects.
- There's no vanilla obsidian platform to land on when you arrive, because the End island is rebuilt. Bring blocks.
- Every kill still drops a dragon egg, which is the only way to get a dragon to raise.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# The Twilight Forest

This is a dimension of permanent dusk with a locked boss chain and its own gear tiers. Two add-ons are installed: Twilight's Flavor & Delight (buff foods, knives, trophy dismantling) and TF Dungeons & Villages (villages that sell rare TF items for Trader Tokens).

This page is the short version. The Twilight Forest has its own official wiki that goes through every boss, structure and item in detail: <http://benimatic.com/tfwiki/index.php/Main_Page>. Use this page for what's different in this pack, and that one for everything else.

## Getting in

1. Dig a pool of water source blocks (a 2×2 works) at ground level in the Overworld.
2. Every block around the rim has to be dirt-type (grass, podzol, moss, and so on) with a plant on top (flower, sapling, grass, fern, mushroom, and so on). Corners count.
3. Throw in a diamond. Lightning strikes (and can start fires in this pack), and the water turns into a portal. The return portal doesn't need a diamond.

If you see "The Portal pool is unresponsive," a rim block or plant is missing. Portals only work from the Overworld.

## Boss chain and biome locks

### Tier 1: Naga to Lich
Both are in the starting biomes. The Lich Tower stays sealed until the Naga dies, and killing the Lich unlocks the Swamp, Snowy Forest and Dark Forest.

### Tier 2: three branches, any order
- Swamp to Labyrinth: kill the Minoshroom and eat Meef Stroganoff to open the Fire Swamp, where the Hydra is.
- Dark Forest: place any trophy on the Trophy Pedestal to summon the Knight Phantoms, then head to the Dark Tower for the Ur-Ghast.
- Snowy Forest: beat the Alpha Yeti, then head to the Glacier for the Snow Queen.

### Tier 3: Highlands and beyond
Beat the Hydra, Ur-Ghast and Snow Queen to clear the Highlands acid rain. Find the Lamp of Cinders in a Troll Cave vault to open the Thornlands. The Final Plateau castle is unfinished in this version of Twilight Forest.

- **Swamp** (needs the Lich dead): locked penalty is Hunger that stacks up every 3 seconds.
- **Snowy Forest** (needs the Lich dead): locked penalty is Frosted III.
- **Dark Forest / Center** (needs the Lich, then the Knight Phantoms): locked penalty is Darkness.
- **Fire Swamp** (needs Meef Stroganoff from the Labyrinth): locked penalty sets you on fire every 3 seconds.
- **Glacier** (needs the Alpha Yeti dead): locked penalty is Frosted IV.
- **Highlands / Thornlands / Final Plateau** (needs Hydra, Ur-Ghast and Snow Queen dead, then the Lamp of Cinders): locked penalty is acid rain that ignores armor, 2 / 4 / 6 HP per second across the three.

## Gear tiers

- **Naga** (chest and legs only): 13 armor, 0.5 toughness. Comes from Naga Scales.
- **Ironwood**: 16 armor, 0 toughness. Sword does 6 damage with 512 durability. Tool level iron. Comes from raw ironwood (liveroot, iron and a gold nugget).
- **Steeleaf**: 20 armor, 0 toughness. Sword does 7 damage with 131 durability. Tool level diamond, and the pickaxe has Fortune II. Comes from Steeleaf leaves.
- **Knightmetal**: 20 armor, 1.0 toughness. Sword does 7 damage with 512 durability and bonus damage against armor. Tool level diamond. Comes from Armor Shards turned into Knightmetal.
- **Phantom**: 20 armor, 2.5 toughness. Comes from the Knight Phantom's chest drop, pre-enchanted.
- **Yeti**: 20 armor, 3.0 toughness. Comes from Alpha Yeti Fur.
- **Arctic**: 16 armor, 2.0 toughness. Comes from Arctic Fur (Winter Wolves, Yetis).
- **Fiery**: 24 armor, 1.5 toughness. Sword does 8 damage with Fire Aspect II and 1024 durability. Tool level netherite, and the pickaxe auto-smelts. Comes from Fiery Blood/Tears plus iron.

## Must-have Twilight items

- Charm of Life I/II (survive a death) and Charm of Keeping I to III (keep your items) go in curio slots. Tier I charms come from Twilight Forest chest loot, then combine upward into higher tiers. The Uncrafting Table, Ore Magnet, Moonworm Queen and Lamp of Cinders can't be crafted: find them in Twilight chests (Hollow Hills, etc.) or buy them with Trader Tokens.
- Ore Magnet: aim at the ground to pull ores to the surface. Moonworm Queen: an infinite light placer. Crumble Horn: comes from the Quest Ram.
- Magic tree cores: Time speeds up growth, Transformation converts biome blocks, Mining auto-mines, and Sorting auto-sorts chests.
- Uncrafting Table: breaks crafted items back into their ingredients.
- Giant Sword / Giant Pickaxe: guaranteed from Armored Giants or Giant Miners in the Highlands. The sword has +2.5 reach.
- Trader Tokens (30% of Twilight Forest chests): spend them in Twilight Villages on scepters, a Triple Bow, Charm of Keeping, the Minotaur Axe, Ore Magnet, Magic Map and more. You can skip a lot of boss RNG this way.

### Twilight's Flavor & Delight
- Buff foods: Plate of Fiery Snakes (Strength III plus Regen II for 5 minutes), Chocolate 113 (Strength V for 30 seconds). Aura effects: Fire, Frozen and Poison Range damage nearby mobs.
- Freezing trick: while under Frozen Range, drop a Bow or Diamond Sword near you and it turns into an Ice Bow or Ice Sword.
- Trophy dismantling on a cutting board: a Knight Phantom trophy gives a Phantom Helmet, and a Snow Queen trophy gives a chance at an Ice Sword, Glass Sword, or Ice Bow.
- Knives: Ironwood (Knockback, Unbreaking), Steeleaf (Looting II, Fortune II), Knightly (bonus vs armor), Fiery (burns, cooks what it cuts).

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Alex's Caves

These are six rare cave biomes hidden under the Overworld, each about 300 blocks wide with its own mobs, boss, materials and signature gear. You won't stumble into most of them by accident. In this pack, Alex's Caves mobs spawn at 1.75x density, and the nuke is 33% bigger than stock.

## How to find them: tablets, codex, map

1. Find an Underground Cabin in normal caves (cartographers and wandering traders sell cabin maps, and mineshaft chests have a 15% chance). Cabins hold Cave Tablets and a Spelunkery Table.
2. Tablets also drop from structures: Bastion 45% (Magnetic), suspicious sand 15% (Primordial), Jungle Temple 50% (Toxic), Underwater Ruins 40% (Abyssal), Woodland Mansion 75% (Forlorn), Witch Hut 90% (Candy).
3. At a Spelunkery Table, decode a tablet with paper (you get 5 attempts before it breaks). Succeed 3 times for the same biome to get a Cave Codex. In this pack, every biome needs its own codex.
4. A codex plus paper makes a Cave Biome Map, which points to the nearest one.

- **Magnetic Caves** (most common, at least 400 blocks out): Tesla bulbs and Boundroids (ceiling ambush) guard it, with Magnetron as the boss. Signature rewards are the Galena Gauntlet, Resistor Shield, magnets, Seeking Arrows and the Magnetic Quarry.
- **Primordial Caves** (common, at least 450 blocks out): dinosaurs, including the 150 HP Tremorsaurus, with the 600 HP Luxtructosaurus as the boss. Signature rewards are the Extinction Spear, dinosaur tames, Primordial armor and the Amber Monolith (repopulates animals).
- **Toxic Caves** (uncommon, at least 650 blocks out): radiation, Nucleepers (explode for up to 100 damage) and Brainiacs, with Tremorzilla as the boss. Signature rewards are the Raygun, Nuclear Bomb, Hazmat suit, Nuclear Furnace and Raycat pets.
- **Abyssal Chasm** (rare, deep ocean only, at least 400 blocks out): pressure darkness and Mine Guardians, with the 400 HP Hullbreaker as the boss. Signature rewards are Deep One trading (Ortholance, Sea Staff, Magic Conch), a diving suit, and the Submarine.
- **Forlorn Hollows** (very rare, at least 650 blocks out): total darkness and Watchers (can possess your camera), with the 250 HP Forsaken as the boss. Signature rewards are the Dreadbow, Hood + Cloak of Darkness (flight), Desolate Dagger and Totem of Possession.
- **Candy Cavity** (rarest, at least 500 blocks out): Gumbeepers and Licowitches, with the rideable Gum Worm as the boss. Signature rewards are the Sugar Staff, Frostmint Spears, Rainbounce Boots, Sack of Sating (auto-feeding) and Sugar Rush (slows time).

## Key mechanics

- Deep One reputation: place Pearls on an Abyssal Altar near Deep Ones. The more they like you, the less they flee, and eventually they'll fight beside you and gift items: Ortholance, Sea Staff and Magic Conch, at about 4% each.
- Submarine: surround an Enigmatic Engine (a Hullbreaker drop) with copper blocks and mark the front with 6 Depth Glass. It gives you air and floodlights. The Torpedoes add-on adds TNT, Nuclear and Pulsar torpedoes.
- Taming: Subterranodon needs Trilocaris tails, Vallumraptor needs Serene Salad after it steals a Dinosaur Nugget, Tremorsaurus needs a stun from the Primitive Club then Serene Salad, Candicorn needs Caramel Apples, and Tremorzilla's egg only hatches from a nuclear blast, then you feed the baby 4 or more Waste Drums.
- Radiation: being Irradiated stops your natural regen and hurts more at higher levels. A full Hazmat suit makes you immune, and Soylent Green helps.
- The Nuclear Bomb in this pack has a 48-block radius, breaks nearly everything (up to 1,000 blast resistance) and drops the items it destroys. A Create mechanical crafter can make one, and the Oppenheimered add-on makes Fissile Cores craftable.
- Create compat: sequenced assembly mass-produces normally rare drops, including Telecore, Polymer Plate, Occult Gem, Notor Gizmo, Depth Charge and Charred Remnant.

> Server hazards: the Totem of Possession works on players in this pack, and a nuke has a 96-block-wide blast. Don't set one off near other people's bases.

## Which cave to try first, and what to bring
Rough order from gentlest to nastiest. It's a suggestion, not a lock: nothing stops you going straight for the Toxic Caves.

1. **Magnetic Caves** first. It's the most common and the closest, and the Galena Gauntlet and Resistor Shield you bring back make the rest easier. Watch the ceilings, because Boundroids drop on you.
2. **Primordial Caves** next. The dinosaurs hit hard but they're ordinary fights, and a tamed Subterranodon or Vallumraptor pays for the trip.
3. **Abyssal Chasm** when you can breathe and see underwater. It's deep ocean only, so bring water breathing or the diving suit, and light, because the pressure darkness is real.
4. **Toxic Caves** only with a full Hazmat suit. Radiation stops your natural healing, and a Nucleeper can do up to 100 damage in one go.
5. **Forlorn Hollows** with plenty of light. It's pitch dark, and Watchers can take over your camera.
6. **Candy Cavity** whenever you find it. It's the rarest, and less dangerous than it is hard to locate.

Every cave takes its own Cave Codex in this pack, so a map to one doesn't help you find another.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

# Space, ships and aircraft

This covers rockets, planets, planes, airships and physics ships.

> Ad Astra is not installed in this pack. Northstar Redux is the whole space system here. Create Planetary Tweaks' rocket and space-suit recipes are dead code (they need Ad Astra), though a few of its tool and armor recipes still work: copper, zinc, brass, steel, and void-wormed.

## Northstar: your first rocket

1. Get to Precision Mechanisms and Electron Tubes in Create.
2. Craft Oxygen Separators, then a Rocket Combustion Chamber, then a Jet Engine (titanium plates plus the chamber plus a Create propeller).
3. Craft a Rocket Station (a 5×5 mechanical crafting recipe: circuits, a targeting computer, a hardened precision mechanism) and Rocket Controls.
4. Build the rocket: hull blocks, Jet Engines (more for heavier rockets), a fuel tank of Methane or Hydrocarbon, a seat, and controls. Superglue it all together.
5. Put a Star Map in the Rocket Station, sit at the controls, and press `Space`. Moons need nothing extra; other planets need an Interplanetary Navigator on board.
6. Wear a space suit. Without oxygen you "forgot your spacesuit."
